Play takes a `acquireContinuous("play")` lease for the session so every overlay blit is preceded by `scene.render()`. Visible editor canvases always render continuously (capped); `syncEditorPlayState(handle, paused)` pauses their schedulers **and** disables their `registerView` clients while overlay Play is open **or** Preview Build is packing (`preparing`) / showing the iframe (`playing`). `editorViewportPausedForSession` is that or. Preview’s iframe is a second Engine — leaving the Scene GLB render loop running on software GL can starve the player of ticks. On close it re-enables the view, resizes (undoing Play’s `setSize`), and invalidates so the docked viewport redraws.

Play HUD `draws` is the last rendered frame’s Babylon draw-call count. `createEngine` resets `engine._drawCalls` before `scene.render()` and stores `_drawCalls.current` afterward (`EngineHandle.drawCalls()`). Babylon 9 has no public `engine.drawCalls` number; reading that field always showed `0`. Overlay SceneLayer instances are extra unlit ortho `Scene`s on the same Engine, drawn after the world (and world PP) so world post-process never hits overlays. Each overlay camera ortho matches that layer’s `layerBounds` (default 32×18) and stretches to the framebuffer. Layers with their own PP render to an RTT then alpha-composite. Detail: [scene-layers.md](scene-layers.md).

Play overlay canvas layout comes from Project Settings. When `render.customResolution` is on (new projects default **1920×1080**) and **Black Bars** is on, Play calls `engine.setSize(width, height)` so the framebuffer is exactly WxH, then **letterboxes** that bitmap with `fitContainedRect` (never `object-fit: fill`) and sets the overlay canvas drawing buffer to the same WxH so the blit is 1:1. Unused overlay space is black. When Black Bars is off, Play does **not** lock the buffer: the canvas fills the overlay and shared-engine Play `resize` snaps that overlay then `engine.setSize` to those pixels (not `engine.resize()`); cameras stay centered at the live aspect (`refreshAuthoredCameraLenses`) with no CSS stretch. The overlay ResizeObserver must **not** call `handle.resize()` while that size is locked — that would undo `setSize`; it only re-runs CSS contain (and calls `resize()` when unlocked). `createEngine` `resize` / `setSize` re-apply authored camera lenses at the new render aspect so orthographic extents stay finite and match the buffer. On Play close, `syncEditorPlayState` restores editor size. `ctx.setRenderResolution(width, height)` requests a `setScalability` resolution patch and overrides WxH for the session only (does not write `project.json`); that live override still letterboxes. When custom resolution is missing or off, **Follow System** (default on existing projects) fills the overlay; when Follow System is off, the canvas is the largest centered rectangle of `aspectWidth:aspectHeight` (default 16:9) with unused overlay space black. Editor viewports and Prefab Preview stay fill-the-panel. Export consumes the same game render size.

Primitive collider dimensions are validated during actor realization. Invalid drafts (including a Circle Radius of zero) report the actor/component in the Scene load failure, so the host stops loading instead of remaining at 20% while native physics rejects the shape. Editor authoring can still display and repair these drafts.

## Scene fog

Authored PBR and Unlit surfaces apply scene fog before output color conversion; PBR applies it after linear lighting and emission, before image processing. Linear, Exponential and Exponential Squared settings update the shader uniforms live in the editor and Play. SceneLayer overlays remain fog-free. Fog color uses the same display-to-linear conversion as surface colors.

## Snapshot apply

- `SnapshotInterpolator` holds the two most recent stable bridge snapshots and can lerp (`sample(α)`). It matches actors by `slotId` rather than buffer index, copies newly spawned slots from the next frame, drops removed slots, and uses normalized shortest-path quaternion interpolation; spawn/despawn reorder cannot blend one actor from another actor's pose. `push` copies only the header and `actorCount` rows into its owned pair, so `sample` never reads past `actorCount` (older rows stay stale). It **ignores unpublished** (zeroed, no `magic`) buffers — the worker's first rAF after `play` copies the seq-lock before any tick, and applying that as `actorCount: 0` despawned `assignMesh` visuals and dropped `assignedMaterialGuids()` (`data-assigned-materials`). A published empty scene (`actorCount: 0` with magic) still despawns. Snapshot absence retires only a slot that a previously **applied** sample contained (`binding.seenSlots`, marked at apply time — an interpolated sample can still omit a slot whose buffer was already pushed); a command-built visual not yet seen survives a stale buffer that predates its `assignMesh`, and explicit `despawn` remains its removal path. Play `activeScene` / `loadScene` calls `clear()` so `sample()` is null until the destination snapshot is published — otherwise a stale pre-swap live set (monotonic slot ids) GCs destination cameras created by `assignMesh`. The live Play loop samples **α = 1** (latest published buffer): Play render and the worker tick share the same ~60 Hz cap, so a time-based α would add up to one tick of visual lag with no smoothing. Time-based interpolation waits until render rate exceeds tick rate. `lastActorPositions` always reads the latest snapshot (e2e / HUD), never an interpolated sample.
- Reuse scratch `Vector3` / `Quaternion`; each mesh owns its frozen world `Matrix`, refreshed from local TRS so actor slots cannot alias one matrix and collapse at the origin.
- A registered view samples once at begin-frame admission and the render loop reuses that sample until end-frame unless a push or command invalidated it (views without a registered view sample in the render loop); `SnapshotInterpolator.sample` decodes slots in place without per-actor allocation; `prepareSnapshot` applies a sampled snapshot only when its identity — `{frameId, α, layoutGeneration}` — differs from the last applied identity, so `applySnapshotToScene`, `playViz.refresh`, the active-camera rebuild and audio sync each run once per applied snapshot. The same `frameId` at a different α is a distinct pose and reapplies. `pushSnapshot`, Play `loadScene` / `activeScene` / `sceneLoading` resets, `snapshotLayout`, and the structural commands (`spawn`, `despawn`, `assignMesh`, `assignMaterial`, `attachToBone`, `animState`) clear the identity so the next sample reapplies. Audio pose writes, bone-attachment poses, `syncSnapshot` and listener sync live inside that apply path rather than render admission, so a frame-cap-skipped draw still updates spatial audio exactly once per applied snapshot.
- Snapshot application first reconciles membership and owning scenes, then updates poses/visibility using reused mesh references. Neither pass changes caller-owned `blockMaterialDirtyMechanism` or `blockfreeActiveMeshesAndRenderingGroups`: their native transitions caused global dirty/cleanup work even for identical samples. Resource and material observables still invalidate strict readiness for structural publication/removal. Equal transforms retain their early-out; changed transforms update off-screen shadow casters. Snapshot preparation timing and attributable native-work counts are isolated in `snapshot-steady-state.test.ts`, separately from render submission timing.
- `skipPointerMovePicking: true` on world / editor scenes (mesh hover off). Overlay SceneLayer Play scenes keep move picking on for hover (`onMouseEnter` / `onMouseLeave`). See [scene-layers.md](scene-layers.md).
- `applyAssignMesh` records `meshKind`, `meshAssetGuid`, and optional `parts[]` per slot and rebuilds the Play mesh via `createPlayMesh` **even when the slot has no mesh yet**. Non-sprite / tilemap / skybox Play visuals (primitives, GLB instances, colliders, helpers) sit in `RENDERING_GROUP.world` with the editor grid so Babylon does not clear depth between a primitive in group 0 and a model in group 1. A single identity visual stays `actor-<slotId>` (snapshot-driven). Multiple or offset parts keep that name as a hidden origin and parent `actor-<slotId>|<componentId>` children at local TRS; non-rendering component parents are skipped to the nearest visual ancestor, matching `EditorSceneSync`. Picking walks parents to the origin. `meshKind: "sprite"` / `"tilemap"` build quads/chunks and bind `ResourceCache` textures from collected `textureBytes` with the sprite alpha-test material (`alphaCutOff` 0.4). Tilemap chunks inherit component sorting as a group and order asset layers internally; the shared alpha-test comparator preserves world depth and cutouts. Parallax stays per layer, and animated `:anim` siblings seek their UVs from simulation time. When `twoD.pixelPerfect` is on, snapshot apply snaps the **Play** camera XY to the pixel grid. `MeshComponent.assetGuid` creates an empty named transform root (`actor-<slotId>` / `editorActor:<id>`, hidden and unpickable). Each Model guid is `LoadAssetContainerAsync`'d **once per Scene** from **packed** `source` bytes (`packedGltfBytes`: `slice` a babasset chunk view so the glTF plugin does not read the whole `.babasset` `ArrayBuffer`) and kept as a template `AssetContainer` (not `addAllToScene`). Loader failures leave that empty named root and log `[render] Model <guid> failed to load` (Material Preview custom mesh falls back to the cube). `instantiateModelsToScene` (keep source names, clone skins) parents a per-actor instance under that root. Synthetic GLB encoders for NullEngine tests live in the non-barrel `glb-test-fixtures.ts` (editor tests and proofs use the `@babylonslate/render/glb-test-fixtures` subpath). `visualMeshes` skips the hidden placeholder and 0-vertex `__root__`. Loader `TEXCOORD_0` UVs stay on every part. `applyModelMaterialSlots` maps each construction material to a Model slot by the glTF `/materials/N` pointer (import order), then by slot name (including same-name clones), then by the next unused index — never by `getChildMeshes()` visit order. Shared construction instances share a slot; distinct glTF materials stay distinct slots. Empty guid = glTF construction. Then `MeshComponent.materialGuid` as a whole-visual override. Snapshot TRS on the named root moves skeleton and skinned meshes. Paused `AnimationGroup`s register on `slotAnimationGroups` stamped with the **Animation** guid (`modelClipAnimationGuids`; fallback Model guid): wrap with `start`/`play` then `pause()` so animatables stay live for `goToFrame` — never `stop()`. Retargeted rows load the source Model GLB from the same per-Scene cache and run `retargetAnimationGroupWithMeshProxy` at rest pose, then pause/seek. The last `animState` is replayed when those groups land. `meshKind: "skybox"` is a **visible** mesh (`CreateBox` + unlit PBR `SKYBOX_MODE`, actor world TRS, `ignoreCameraMaxZ`, `isPickable` always false, no shadows); it is not a Play helper. `meshKind: "text3d"` is a **visible** flat mesh (`CreateTextShapePaths` + `CreatePolygon` + injected `earcut`, one unlit two-sided material, bundled ASCII TypeFace when no Font facetype chunk); optional `text3d` on the command and on `parts[]`. `meshKind: "rigidbody"` is a Play helper (`playHelperVisual`) — never a visible 0.25 cube. `meshKind` starting with `collider:` builds the same world-space dashed meshes as the editor (`createColliderVisualMesh`); runtime emits that kind only when `ColliderComponent.renderInGame` is true. Console `showcollision` stays a separate global overlay (`play-console-viz`). `meshKind: "light:*"` / `"camera"` create detached authored lights/`UniversalCamera`s; `assignMesh.light` / `.camera` apply color, intensity, range, cone, enabled, projection, FOV, clips, and Default Camera (`isDefault`). Play cameras and lights use **composed** actor × first-part TRS (the same allocation-free `composeActorComponentTransformToRef` core as the editor for unattached actors; editor lights and cameras on attached actors compose the component over the parent-resolved `authoredActorMatrices` world pose) and zero UniversalCamera Euler after setting `rotationQuaternion`. Missing Default Camera keeps the Play viewport camera named `"camera"` — it does not steal the first CameraComponent. `possessCamera` switches the global Play `activeCamera`; `setScalability` updates the affected quality groups without resetting simulation.
- `applyAssignMaterial` records `materialAssetGuid` per slot (and optional `componentId`) and binds the compiled NodeMaterial from a scene-local `MaterialLibrary`. Overlay slots resolve against the mesh’s Scene with `unlit: true` so SceneLayer `2DMaterial` stays a HUD compile instead of a world-lit material. `assignedMaterialGuids()` lists unique recorded guids for Play e2e (`data-assigned-materials`). `createEngine` loads Material / Material Function documents, resolves textures through `ResourceCache`, and rebuilds the camera `postProcessStack` on `loadScene`, document updates, Engine Settings `postProcessingEnabled`, and whenever Play `activeCamera` identity changes (`assignMesh` Default Camera, `possessCamera`, snapshot spawn/despawn). That local gate defaults on and skips attachment without mutating the scene or exported games. Scene Depth / Scene Normal probe honestly (depth try/catch, keep a pre-existing pre-pass), compile each pass first, then lease only the linearized depth renderer or pre-pass a successful pass needs. Detach releases only buffers this stack created. The packaged player hydrates packed Material / Material Function JSON and the startup stack and forwards `assignMaterial`. `changescene` / `ctx.changeScene` emit `activeScene`; Play hosts look up that guid and call `loadScene` plus `applySceneEnvironment`. Play-mode `loadScene` applies environment (clear / fog / IBL) and the camera post-process stack, **clears the snapshot interpolator**, and retires world Play slot visuals/cameras (`retirePlayWorldSlots`; overlay compositor slots stay) so leftover `possessedCameraSlotId` cannot outrank the destination Default Camera. It does not run `applySceneToBabylonScene`, so a scene change cannot plant document `authoredLight:<actorId>` lights beside Play `assignMesh` lights. `despawn` also retires that slot immediately (same as snapshot prune) instead of waiting for GC. Editor Play also closes material documents over every scene in the Play library so the destination stack can compile. Per-scene navmesh reload is a separate navigation follow-up.
- `applyPossessCamera` outranks the Default Camera: `refreshPlayActiveCamera` prefers the possessed slot, then the `isDefault` slot, then the Play fallback camera named `"camera"`. Snapshot despawn **and Play `despawn` / `loadScene`** call the same refresh so a disposed possessed camera does not leave Babylon’s `cameras[0]` (often the fallback orbit camera) as the game view. Automatic `attemptPossessViewTarget` is a fallback only when the scene has no valid Default Camera; explicit script possession still outranks that default. The option and the graph node both arrive as the same `possessCamera` command ([engineplan §2.5](../engineplan.md)). `changeScene` re-evaluates Attempt Possess on the destination world. Possess after realize only works if `assignMesh` already created the UniversalCamera — an empty `binding.cameras` map used to leave Play on the orbit fallback.
- Post-process buffer admission uses the fully resolved plan from `MaterialLibrary.acquire`, including nested Material Functions. Its optional `validatePlan` gate runs before compilation or acquiring a cached reference; successful acquisitions return that same plan for depth/normal leases. Unsupported passes report a namespaced sampling-node diagnostic and leave neighboring passes attached. Attachment does not lower a second graph with different function context.
- One slot is one visual. Two mesh actors keep two slots and two visible meshes; overlapping meshes are authored co-location, not a lost slot. Place Actors and Outliner drag-duplicate write an explicit world position (view center or drop ray) so a new actor is not planted inside the camera lens or the default Actor origin; authors can still stack two meshes on purpose.
- `animState` applies **all** weighted `layers[]` (or one synthesized layer from `clipName`). One resolved group is written immediately; two or more GLB groups blend by weight in the Scene's next animation pass (the latest command per slot wins, layers sharing a clip merge their weights, and a channel only some clips animate mixes toward rest). Bone attachments read the pre-blend pose during a crossfade. Raw groups reached only through the scene-wide fallback keep last-layer-wins. The deferred blend relies on a pinned Babylon 9.29 pending-data adapter in `glb-anim.ts`. Per-slot `getAnimationGroup(slotId, clipName, clipAssetGuid)` wins over `scene.animationGroups`. A set `clipAssetGuid` must equal the group’s **Animation** guid (native stamp or retargeted row); an untagged or other-clip `"Idle"` does not steal the seek. `clipKind: "sprite"` bakes clip UVs through `applySpriteAnimFrame` when `createEngine({ spritePayloads })` has the sprite asset; two-layer sprite blends use the base mesh plus an overlay (visibilities = weights). Pending `animState` is replayed after `assignMesh` and after GLB `AnimationGroup`s finish loading. See [anim-graph.md](anim-graph.md).
- Editor and Play pass `setMeshAssets` / `CreateEngineOptions.textureBytes` + `modelBytes` + `modelPayloads` collected from scene component guids (`resolveGpuTexture`: preferred `ktx2:<hash>` then source, with editor LOD downsample of PNG when the matching KTX2 is missing; Particle usage binds only a block-aligned KTX2 — the selected one, else `payload.ktx2ChunkId` — otherwise source; export uses authored `payload.ktx2ChunkId` only, packing `pixels` instead for uncompressed Usages or a misaligned Particle KTX2 ([exporter](exporter.md))). Overlay Play feeds those **selected texture bytes** into the **same Engine** the Scene viewport already warmed. Preview Build’s iframe is a **cold Engine** plus packed **PNG/pixels** (`shouldPackKtx2ForPreviewBuild` is always false) so hardware Preview matches overlay Play. Itch **Export Game** still packs **KTX2** when the player has the transcoder (`playerFilesHaveKtx2Transcoder`). Play-mode Engines decode packed KTX2 on the **main thread** (`DefaultNumWorkers = 0`) while retaining supported ASTC/BC7; missing capabilities and software renderers select RGBA. Editor/overlay can use the matching KTX2 variant or source PNG LOD. `fontFacetypeBytes` is the Font `facetype-glyphs` chunk (or export `FontFacetype` sidecar) for `Text3DComponent.fontAssetGuid`. Model `source` bytes slim embedded rasters to a 1×1 red PNG **only** when every slot has a `materialGuid`, that Material **compiled** in this Scene (`compiledMaterialGuids`), **and** every texture guid those Materials sample exists in `textureBytes` (`shouldSlimModelEmbeddedTextures`). Packed bytes alone must not slim — Preview otherwise leaves construction mats on the red stub. Missing packed Texture bytes keep the authored GLB PNG/JPEG. `compileMaterialPlan` assigns `TextureBlock.texture` immediately (so `applyModelMaterialSlots` stays sync) then **rebuilds** `NodeMaterial.build()` when the texture is not **sample-ready** (`isGpuTextureSampleReady`: not `loadingError`, not `engine.emptyTexture`, and `isReady()`) and `onLoadObservable` fires — **unfreeze → build → re-apply blend → markDirty with dirty-block off → restore freeze** so editor `material.freeze()` and Play snapshot `blockMaterialDirtyMechanism` cannot keep the error sampler. Particle-mode Materials skip this rebuild ([particles.md](particles.md)). Texture load failure reports `material.missingTexture` (`onErrorObservable` or `loadingError` at bind). Overlay PNG decode is effectively sync. Play/player `createEngine({ playMode: true })` **skips** `applyEditorMaterialFreeze` after `loadScene` / `setMaterialDocuments` / `prewarmSceneMaterials` so a cold iframe Engine can rebuild when packed textures land. Play Intermediate still sets `isFrozen` (`checkReadyOnlyOnce`) when the NodeMaterial first becomes ready; the load callback unfreezes around `build()`. The packaged player waits for GLB instances (`whenEditorModelsReady`) then **sample-ready textures** (`whenMaterialTexturesReady`) then `prewarmSceneMaterials`. `prewarmMaterial` no-ops until TextureBlocks are sample-ready. Pack warns when a Material samples a Texture with no bytes.

## Render-on-demand

Visible editor canvases (scene viewport and Prefab Preview) always render continuously at Engine Settings `viewportFrameCap` (default 30). Freeze — skip `scene.render()` — when the canvas is zero-size or fully off-screen, a dialog/alert/sheet overlay is open, Play is up, the app is backgrounded, or the canvas integer size is changing (`setResizing` until `CANVAS_RESIZE_HOLD_MS` after the last tick). IntersectionObserver is the fast path; if it reports not intersecting, a window-overlap `getBoundingClientRect` check still keeps an on-screen canvas visible (iOS standalone IO is unreliable under Dockview CSS transforms). Dirty-driven invalidation and refcounted continuous-render leases still honor the same frame cap (Play holds a continuous lease). Play views use the project `playFrameCap` (default 60) rather than the editor viewport cap. The cap is applied when Play starts from Project Settings `playFrameCap`. Live Engine Settings changes (`applyLiveEngineSettings`) must **not** copy `viewportFrameCap` onto that Play scheduler (`{ applyFrameCap: false }` in the overlay). The overlay HUD shows FPS / script / physics and does not change the cap live. HUD exposes actual rendered FPS; invalidations/sec remain available in scheduler diagnostics.

**Editor scene freezes + shader warm** (`p20-editor-scene-freeze`, **Done**): editor scenes set `ScenePerformancePriority.BackwardCompatible` (Play stays `Intermediate` + `autoClear = true`). Editor scenes keep world-matrix and material freezes but never freeze the active-mesh queue: the FrameGraph collects its own objects and rejects a frozen queue, so brush strokes, gizmo edits and model instantiation need no unfreeze/refreeze. Grid hide keeps `isVisible` true and drives a `gridVisible` shader uniform (`ShaderMaterial` ignores `mesh.visibility`). `freezeWorldMatrix()` on static actor meshes (not skybox / billboard / grid); Play snapshot apply uses the same `shouldFreezeStaticWorldMatrix` skip so skybox meshes keep their authored world pose; GLB adopt re-freezes the actor root after TRS. `material.freeze()` on compiled surface NodeMaterials and the engine default except open Material tabs (`setEditingMaterialGuids`; particle-domain materials stay unfrozen). Play/player `playMode` **does not** call `applyEditorMaterialFreeze` after `loadScene` / `setMaterialDocuments` / `prewarmSceneMaterials` — pending packed textures must be able to rebuild (Play Intermediate still auto-sets `isFrozen` on first ready). Editor freeze stays. `SCENE_LOOKUP_MAPS` on `createEngine` scene construct, and `prewarmSceneMaterials` / `forceCompilationAsync` on viewport remount (**Warming Shaders**). Do **not** dirty-skip a visible editor viewport.

`adaptToDeviceRatio: false`; resolution via `setHardwareScalingLevel`. Engine Settings `hardwareScalingLevel` (default 1) is both the **initial** level and the valve **floor** (`minLevel`). The valve must not hunt below that into supersampling (`minLevel: 0.25` is 16× pixels). `setLevel` is a no-op when the clamped value is unchanged. `targetFrameMs` comes from the view’s cap (`1000/frameCap`, else `1000/60`). The editor viewport test host exposes `hardwareScalingLevel` and `postProcessPassCount` so e2e can assert the live gate. Pause render loop, game worker, and encode queue on background.

The dynamic resolution valve (`HardwareScalingController.noteFramePressure`) is fed a per-view `FramePressureSample` at each presented (non-loading) frame: the presented-frame interval measured between consecutive engine end-frames (`null` whenever the view did not present the previous frame — capped/skipped, hidden or obstructed frames break the chain and never contribute a zero), the cost of `scene.render()` itself timed immediately around that call — never the wall-clock gap since the previous rendered frame, which a frozen obstructed viewport can leave at seconds — and the engine GPU frame-time counter only when the view is the Engine's sole rendering view (owned Engine, or the single enabled registered view). Shared-Engine siblings report `gpuMs: null` with `gpuAttribution: "shared-engine"`; an unsupported or still-empty timer reports `"unavailable"`. The 15-sample median of `max(presentationMs, cpuMs, gpuMs)` scales down past the hysteresis margin; scale-up requires known on-target presentation intervals across the whole window plus CPU — and, when attributable, GPU — headroom. Presentation/GPU signals are reported only by `playMode` handles; free-running editor/prefab viewports report `presentationMs`/`gpuMs` as `null` (their presented-frame interval measures host event-loop contention, not frame cost), so their valve input is the cpuMs-only contract and a window with no presentation evidence falls back to the CPU/GPU headroom rule. Live Engine Settings changes call `setSettingsLevel` so the new value is both the current level and the floor — cheap frames must not hunt back below the user's choice.

Scene, Prefab, and Play canvases resize only when the integer CSS size actually changes (`createCanvasResizeGuard`). Zero-size updates are ignored so a hidden tab cannot thrash `engine.resize()`. Each integer change **snaps the drawing buffer to integer CSS pixels** (`snapCanvasDrawingBuffer` in `canvas-drawing-buffer.ts`) so a skipped frame is blank at the host aspect instead of CSS-stretching the last framebuffer. Shared Scene and Play views (`sharedEngine` + `registerView`) call `engine.setSize` from the **view** CSS box — not `engine.resize()`, which follows the hidden project constructor canvas — even when `registerView` is skipped because the view canvas cannot take a 2D blit. Prefab RTT `clear()` uses the same snap helper. `RenderScheduler.setResizing` then skips `scene.render` until the size has been stable for `CANVAS_RESIZE_HOLD_MS` (80ms, longer than a typical DockView sash tick) so slow dock drags stay blank instead of flickering. Scene and Prefab canvas hosts use `min-h-0 min-w-0` so DockView sashes and document tabs change the canvas box rather than overflowing a stretched bitmap. Do **not** move Scene to RTT — Play `registerView` still blits from the Scene Engine framebuffer.

Material Preview (third Scene on the shared Engine) presents RTT-only: `scene.autoClear = true` clears the camera `outputRenderTarget` every frame (not the shared default framebuffer), sticky 2D canvas width/height assigns only when changed, the coordinator's render skips while frozen or a blit is in flight, rAF cancels when frozen, and present caps to Engine Settings `viewportFrameCap` (default 30). The 2D blit uses `flipReadPixelsRgba` like Prefab / Model / Animation previews. Custom mesh preview `setMesh("custom", bytes)` `LoadAssetContainerAsync` of **packed** bytes (`packedGltfBytes` / `slice` of a babasset `source` view) + instantiate under a hidden placeholder (same as Model Preview), and `applyMaterial` paints `visualMeshes` — not a first-primitive stub. `aimPreviewCameraAtMesh` / Model Preview radius use that visual AABB (skip the hidden placeholder and empty `__root__`). Missing bytes or a swallowed loader error stay a cube. It must not `registerView` or default-framebuffer `scene.render()` while Scene or Play owns the framebuffer. Disposing that preview Scene (canvas remount, idle-unmount, context restore) must not leave a white untextured cube: `MaterialEditingProvider` recompiles on a new scene epoch, `MaterialLibrary.acquire` skips disposed NodeMaterials, and `ResourceCache.getTexture` rebuilds disposed textures so Texture Parameter samples stay bound. Render after that restore uses the new GPU objects — closing the tab is not required. A second `ResourceCache` (512MiB ceiling) or slot `MaterialLibrary` on a thumbnail Scene can lose the shared context and bump `previewSceneEpoch`.

Preview presenters wait for visible-mesh shader/texture readiness before consuming their frame interval, retain forced redraws requested during a pending pixel read, and discard readback after disposal. Model previews keep a 0.01 near clipping plane inside their 0.25 minimum orbit radius, so small or import-scaled models are visible when first framed.

Interactive previews (Model, Animation, Skeleton, Material, opt-in Particle) share the engine default skybox through `installPreviewEnvironment` (`packages/render/src/preview-environment.ts`): `createSkyboxMesh` resolves the same cube the default Scene's SkyboxComponent uses, so one uploaded texture serves every preview rather than a per-scene copy. The skybox is background chrome — it draws as soon as its faces load and stays out of preview readiness gating so it cannot hold the first presented frame. Prefab Preview opts out (black clear); thumbnails and hidden retarget hosts never call the helper. Preview presenters report render and readback failures through `onError` — Material Editing dispatches `previewError` into the preview status (`material-preview-error`) and Model Preview sets its load-error banner — instead of silently swallowing them. Readbacks are serialized by `blitInFlight`; a completed read is dropped when the presenter was disposed or its RTT generation was superseded, so an older frame can never overwrite the canvas after a newer generation started. Babylon's WebGPU texture manager already de-pads the 256-byte-aligned `copyTextureToBuffer` rows before `readPixels` resolves, so `flipReadPixelsRgba` receives tight RGBA and flips exactly once on both backends. The editor-host browser proof records sixty presented viewport frames per backend — skybox pixel stability at fixed points, imported-model realization through the editor sync, material readiness, culling state and console/GPU validation errors — repeats after a WebGL2 → WebGPU project switch, and captures the real Material, Model and Prefab document previews (non-blank readback, skybox presence, studio light rig, zero shadow generators, CEL replacement reach) under both render modes.

Play frame pacing uses a timestamp captured before render work, committed only after a successful render. The scheduler advances a stable deadline with at most 1 ms (and at most 5% of the interval) of early-callback tolerance; cap changes and long suspensions rebase it without catch-up bursts. Render-cost measurements for dynamic resolution remain separate. Scheduler statistics normalize completed renders and invalidations by actual elapsed time; the Play and player FPS readouts use rendered FPS, independently of input callbacks and simulation ticks.

## Resource cache

Content Browser Material and Actor Prefab captures use `captureAssetThumbnailPng`: a transparent 128px RTT on the project Engine, a sphere for surface Materials, and `EditorSceneSync` for saved prefab geometry. Captures frame visible authored meshes (excluding editor helpers), wait for models/materials, and never start a render loop or execute gameplay scripts. Their scoped MaterialLibrary borrows texture leases from the existing Engine ResourceCache; cancellation, failure and successful readback all release temporary scene, material and target resources. Model/Animation captures retain their construction-GLB path. See [thumbnail caching and scheduling](asset-registry.md#thumbnails).

Imported ENV and linear-float prefiltered DDS cubes use the retained source with an explicit `.env`/`.dds` loader extension, including Blob URLs in Scene, Play and packed assets. Native CubeTexture prefiltered loading preserves roughness mips, linear decoding and available irradiance; it does not perform authoring convolution. Cache-owned cubes belong to the Engine and are shared without per-scene texture mutations. Ordinary 2D material bindings reject cube data before allocating a sampler. [Supported imports and metadata](asset-registry.md#importers) describe the bounded format contract. Scene environment lighting and raw Scene cube sampling follow the contracts below; general cube Texture Parameters remain separate work.

The additive `render.environmentLighting` project contract and sparse `Scene.settings.environmentLighting` overrides preserve existing IBL with Enabled=true, Intensity=1, Rotation Y=0°. CEL Strength defaults to zero. Disabled settings retain the selected cube and authored values; removing a scene override resumes live project inheritance. These settings are independent of clear color, fog and PBR/CEL mode.

The scene environment controller leases one cache source and clones only its CubeTexture view, retaining the same radiance InternalTexture and optional irradiance resource. Rotation changes the view matrix, and intensity uses `scene.iblIntensity` once. Explicit raw Environment Sample consumers keep the resource admitted when automatic IBL is disabled, with automatic intensity zero and raw texels unchanged. Repeated identical source collection reuses the view; swaps, disablement and scene disposal release its source lease. Pending and failed native uploads participate in scene readiness.

Authored PBR graphs emit their optional Scene reflection branch during the initial build, even before asset collection supplies a cube. Babylon 9.29 otherwise omits that code when ReflectionBlock has no texture at build time. The coordinate helper supplies a finite unused vector when `REFLECTION` is disabled, so both GLSL and WGSL remain compilable before assignment and after removal. Reflection sampler declarations follow the same define so WebGPU does not require an unbound resource for an inactive branch. Native texture defines and binding still keep the branch disabled while no environment is admitted; late assignment and live scalar edits need no dummy cube or graph rebuild. Actual browser proofs cover these transitions on the same frozen graph.

Supplied environment irradiance coefficients are preserved. If coefficients are absent, one guarded preparation per uploaded cube derives diffuse spherical polynomials from base radiance. Cubes up to 32×32 read mip zero directly; larger cubes use a 32×32 float/half-float target with sixteen stratified mip-zero samples per cell. Samples are decoded to linear radiance before averaging, so authored GGX roughness mips cannot blur the diffuse input. This bounded quadrature can miss small high-frequency features and is less precise than supplied coefficients. Missing float-render or explicit-mip capability fails readiness visibly. The offscreen pass restores shared Engine drawing state before awaiting readback. Scene-owned views expose completed coefficients without invoking Babylon 9.29's disposal-unsafe lazy getter. Readiness includes preparation and readback failure; source and temporary target leases remain held until all submitted reads settle, and disposed or replaced views receive no stale completion.

CEL Environment Strength defaults to zero. When enabled, rotated diffuse irradiance joins the existing strongest/additive/blend light accumulation before its final CEL band calculation, for native/imported and graph surfaces; it contributes no smooth specular reflection. The Material Output Environment Influence pin (default one, clamped to 0–1) scales only indirect PBR contributions or that CEL environment contribution. Direct lighting and authored emission remain independent. Native imported PBR materials retain their own environment intensity multiplier when adapted to CEL.

Environment Sample reads the current Scene cube in Surface or Post Process fragment graphs, including through Material Functions. Direction is a world-space cube direction transformed by Scene orientation; a zero or near-zero direction uses world +Z. Roughness is clamped to 0–1 and follows Babylon 9.29's normal-incidence PBR prefilter mapping: `log2(width × (roughness² + 0.0005))`, or `log2(width) × roughness` for linear-specular cubes, then the cube's LOD generation scale/offset and mip bounds. Raw sampling has no view-normal, anisotropy or geometric-AA adjustment. Its output is raw decoded linear radiance, independent of IBL enablement, intensity and Material Output influence. A missing Scene cube produces black. To author a replacement environment contribution through emission, set Environment Influence to zero; sampling itself does not add an automatic lighting term. Each compiled, enabled consumer keeps the Scene cube leased through material rebuilds and releases it on disposal. WebGL2/WebGPU explicit mip sampling is required; Particle graphs and ordinary 2D Texture Parameter pins retain their existing contracts.

Scene Preview collects its environment GUID; Play acquires environment sources only for currently required consumers. Asset/export callers supply `Uint8Array`, whose container is detected from bytes. Direct `Blob` cube callers must supply `application/vnd.babylon.env` or `image/vnd-ms.dds`; unknown/empty MIME is rejected before allocation because the synchronous cache cannot inspect an arbitrary Blob. Ordinary 2D Texture selection still prioritizes canonical `pixels`; environment selection requires the retained `source` regardless of chunk ordering.

KTX2 format detection and dimension reads share the standard "KTX 20" identifier ([Khronos specification](https://registry.khronos.org/KTX/specs/2.0/ktxspec.v2.html#_identifier)). This routes real encoded atlases through Babylon’s KTX2 loader in Scene Preview and Play and preserves texture byte accounting. Pixel Art usage selects source pixels even when the asset retains an older compressed chunk.

The GPU upload copy normalizes legacy Zstd UASTC descriptors with zero `bytesPlane` fields to the 16-byte UASTC block size. The vendored Basis encoder emits that older representation, which the [KTX specification requires readers to support](https://registry.khronos.org/KTX/specs/2.0/ktxspec.v2.html#_dfd_for_supercompressed_data), but Babylon KTX2 decoders before 9.29 otherwise decode empty image slices. The vendored 9.29 decoder handles it; the normalization remains for previously cached decoder scripts. Original asset bytes and cache identity remain intact; no asset migration is required.

The texture LRU has a byte ceiling (Engine Settings, default **2 GB** accounted, toggleable) and exact-generation lease counts. Unreferenced entries trim toward **80%** of the ceiling. Each installed content generation retains its source Blob and a canonical object URL; distinct native upload requirements receive additional object URLs from the same immutable Blob. Alpha interpretation and anisotropy can use independent wrappers over a compatible native upload. Never fragment `blob:` URLs: Babylon file loading escapes the fragment into the resource identity. KTX2 loader selection uses the explicit MIME and forced-extension options. All source URLs retire with their exact cache entry.

**One `ResourceCache` per Engine lifetime.** `resourceCacheForEngine(engine)` shares compatible uploads between world, SceneLayer and preview scenes. `acquireTexture`, `acquireBlobUrl`, and `acquireCubeTextureFromImages` return exact-generation leases with idempotent `release()`; reading a held resource never acquires again. `ResourceCacheOwner` tracks only outstanding view leases as a teardown safety net. Materials remain scene-owned. Sprite/text/particle bindings, material generations, skybox face URLs, environment views and pending irradiance preparation release their own leases. Native upload pins survive an early owner release and settle on completion/failure. Lease readiness includes byte-budget admission after native upload accounting; a rejected successor releases only its provisional wrapper. A failed first environment upload releases its binding while retaining a readiness diagnostic; unchanged frames do not retry it. Unreferenced generations follow the existing byte accounting/LRU policy; live resources are never evicted to admit a replacement.

Texture installation copies mutable arrays into immutable Blobs and computes SHA-256 once (`installAssetBytes` / `installedAssetIdentity`). Reinstalling changed bytes creates a new identity even when GUID and byte length match; equal installed content shares uploads across views. External immutable Blobs receive an explicit generation. Sampling variants include orientation, mipmaps, sampler mode, sRGB upload, cube/2D and alpha interpretation. KTX2/environment MIME and retained source bytes remain available for reconstruction. Material generation keys include installed texture identities; compiler callbacks borrow their generation's leases.

Sprite quads use updatable position/UV buffers and retain CPU arrays. Reapplying a frame preserves material and texture ownership; UV, size, pivot and blend visibility updates are independent. Texture successors prepare before replacing a working binding, and a failed request is not retried every frame. Auto-generated sprite materials remain stable; animation preserves an authored material assignment. The test-only texture lease proof replays 10,000 selections using real browser buffers/materials and checks atlas pixels and independent SceneLayer ownership.

Cache key includes `url`, `noMipmap`, `samplingMode`, `invertY`, `isCube` (cache textures never use an sRGB buffer). A later `acquireTexture` with a **different** upload key builds another GPU wrapper from a second object URL of the same Blob and does not dispose the first. That keeps a 3D Mannequin albedo (`invertY: false`) upright when a sprite/tilemap requests NEAREST / no mips / `invertY: true`. GPU-dead wrappers (no Babylon `isDisposed()` on Texture — scene-owned wrappers lose `getScene()`, engine-owned ones lose `_engine`) are still treated as a miss and rebuilt from the retained blob URL. Sprite / tilemap `applyAlbedoTexture` requests `hasAlpha` as an immutable pixel-art wrapper option so diffuse alpha participates in the final alpha-test cutout; glTF / NodeMaterial wrappers keep their own alpha settings. Compiled NodeMaterials detach engine-owned `TextureBlock` textures before `dispose(false, false)` so overlay / MaterialLibrary teardown cannot drop ResourceCache GPU objects. Pixel-perfect `applyPixelArtSamplingToScene` skips engine-owned cache textures (`uniqueId` unset) **and** PBR/GLB construction albedos (CLAMP would break Mannequin UVs). `acquireTexture(..., { isCube: true }).resource` returns a `CubeTexture` (IBL, single DDS/ENV URL). `acquireCubeTextureFromImages(guid, scene, files).resource` builds a six-face skybox cube (`files` in `px, py, pz, nx, ny, nz` order) **on the Engine** (`new CubeTexture(files, engine)`), not the Scene, so Play `scene.dispose()` cannot drop a cache-owned cube. Constructing `Texture` outside the cache is lint-banned. Skybox empty faces load `engine-content/skybox/{px,py,pz,nx,ny,nz}.png` (Vite-copied to editor/player public; URL helper in `packages/render/src/default-skybox/`); override Texture guids are collected into Play `textureBytes` (`skyboxFaceGuidsFromScene`). The skybox never sets `scene.environmentTexture`. Editor billboard PNGs live in `engine-content/billboards/` (Vite-copied to **editor** `public/` only; URL helper `engineBillboardUrl` in `packages/render/src/default-billboard/`). Player does not copy them.

**KTX2 orientation.** Babylon 9.29's block-compressed (ASTC/BC7) uploads ignore `invertY`: WebGPU passes `false` and WebGL2 never sets `UNPACK_FLIP_Y_WEBGL`. Its KTX2 loader also sets no `_invertVScale`, unlike KTX1 and `.basis`. Without compensation, a compressed KTX2 at an `invertY: true` site drew upside down against its PNG.
- `prepareTexture` builds a 2D KTX2 requested with `invertY` (every `applyAlbedoTexture` site: Sprite, Sprite Animation, Tilemap, 2D Texture, 2D Panel with a texture source, rich-text `[img]`) with `invertY: false` and flips V on the wrapper (`vScale = -1`, `vOffset = 1`, so v′ = 1 − v). It decides from the bytes (KTX2 magic or `image/ktx2` Blob), so the result is upright whether the decoder picks a block format or RGBA. PNG and `invertY: false` requests (Materials, glTF) are unchanged; nothing is re-encoded.
- The flip lives on each wrapper, set at construction: cache reuse, sampling variants (`acquireTextureVariant`) and device-loss rebuilds (which reuse the InternalTexture's `invertY: false`) keep it. Upload keys keep the *requested* `invertY`, so flipped and unflipped wrappers never share one; `Texture.invertY` reads `false` on flipped ones.
- Samplers must apply the texture matrix, as StandardMaterial, the shared outline mask, shadow/depth passes and render-target alpha masks do. A Babylon upgrade that sets `_invertVScale = !invertY` for KTX2 would flip twice; `= invertY` is a no-op here.
- Measured on SwiftShader WebGPU (block-compressed and RGBA) and on software WebGL2 (RGBA, flipped by the GLSL StandardMaterial's texture matrix). Hardware WebGL2's compressed path is inferred from code only: CI's software GL decodes every KTX2 to RGBA.

Texture storage is estimated from each loaded sampling representation's actual GPU format and type, rounded compressed blocks, individual mip dimensions, and cube faces. Pending uploads reserve RGBA8 when source dimensions are available; Blob header reads are bounded to 64 KiB. KTX2 base dimensions and level count come from its header because Babylon 9.29's RGBA fallback leaves internal dimensions at the last mip. Partial KTX2 chains count only their stored levels, including levels uploaded despite a no-mip sampling request. Load/dispose callbacks belong to the exact cache entry and wrapper; eviction and external disposal remove their accounting and observers. These estimates exclude driver alignment and hidden allocations, and are not measured VRAM. WebGL restore logs lost/restored to the Output Log and resets hardware scaling to the Engine Settings floor (`noteRestore`). Babylon rebuilds retained GPU resources before notifying; handle callbacks preserve those shared textures and materials. Disposal removes the handle's restore/loss/frame observers and rejects pending presentation. Context loss invalidates outstanding load and shader-warm generations.

Invariant: Play open-and-close must not grow `engine.getLoadedTexturesCache().length` (asserted in Play stop + unit cache cycle). Playwright repeats three real Play sessions and checks texture counts stay equal while asynchronous mesh counts remain bounded.

Per-Scene GLB containers (`glb-anim.ts`) account GPU vertex+index bytes (`accountedGeometryBytesForScene`), once per unique container geometry so instanced glTF parts are not counted twice. The HUD shows `geo` and **Geo High** when that exceeds `GEOMETRY_BYTE_CEILING` (512 MB). Geometry is **not** LRU-evicted and has **no** Engine Setting.

An authored sprite Material temporarily overrides the retained automatic sprite material; it does not retire that construction material or its exact texture lease. Clearing the assignment restores the automatic material immediately, including static sprites. Animation requests resume its binding after the clear. A pending texture completion can update the retained automatic material but cannot replace a newer authored assignment. Mesh retirement disposes its owned material and releases its lease without disposing the borrowed authored Material.

Material generation publication and texture parameter replacement/reset wait for the owned texture lease's complete preparation, including delayed byte accounting and budget admission. Babylon's native `Texture.isReady()` alone does not establish that contract. A rejected or superseded preparation keeps the prior material/parameter and its leases; cancellation settles the material's readiness without awaiting an abandoned header read. Compiler shader readiness remains separate from this library-owned publication gate.

Pending texture preparation has a 30-second readiness deadline and is cancelled when its generation loses its final texture/source-URL owner. Cancellation or expiry rejects that preparation, retires its provisional wrapper and releases its cache pin; a remaining owner prevents last-owner cancellation. GPU wrapper retirement also settles pending header work so restoration can reacquire the retained source. Late upload/header callbacks cannot publish, account bytes or run admission after retirement.

Fractional sprite animation weights use alpha test plus blending on the owned automatic material; full and hidden weights use cutout mode. Babylon 9.29's explicit alpha-test mode otherwise bypasses visibility-based blend classification. Classification boundaries invalidate strict readiness once per affected material; changes within a fractional fade reuse the material, buffers, leases and readiness result. Authored materials retain their own alpha mode. The existing two-layer source-over composition and sorting remain unchanged; this does not introduce a normalized crossfade shader.

A sprite texture replacement with a working binding waits for the successor lease's complete readiness promise, including asynchronous byte accounting and budget admission. Native `Texture.isReady()` alone cannot publish over or release the predecessor. Failed admission retires only the candidate and preserves the working binding; a same-request failure remains latched until its source changes.

Replacing a texture-bearing skybox, sprite or tilemap visual prepares a disabled candidate hierarchy while retaining the prior visual. Publication waits for exact owned texture leases, including upload/admission failure, and uses the existing owner/generation cancellation paths. Play stages its crossfade overlay and native helpers separately; failed or superseded candidates cannot replace those live maps. The winning root moves external emitter users before old-root retirement, then replays the latest animation state and invalidates snapshot preparation. Failed requests are latched until their descriptor changes. A failed first skybox leaves a disabled placeholder and releases its owned material and cube lease, avoiding abandoned readiness work.

The same Play replacement boundary prepares model-bearing multipart hierarchies with an independent loading epoch and animation lookup per part. Every model and owned texture must prepare before the root, overlay and complete animation lookup publish together. Current-owner model failures reach the stored slot load while preserving the prior hierarchy. A first multipart load uses the existing model placeholder until preparation succeeds. Removed component parameter-material owners are retained with the working visual and released only after successful retirement or owner disposal. Material assignment/parameter commands restart an exact pending preparation, including in-place models, so a late command cannot bypass its material readiness gate. During that replacement, the old visual keeps its private material owner; GUID-filtered retirement cannot release the successor's different GUID. Repeated material changes retain at most the working owner per component key, release superseded candidate owners after cancellation, and release all owners on slot/binding teardown. Standalone material-assignment replacement remains outside this pending-visual transaction change. Single-model replacement uses this same commit path; in-place model completion also invalidates snapshot identity so bone attachment matrices refresh in the same sample. Bone attachments keep matrices rather than Babylon parent links, and survive model-node retirement.

## Simultaneous lights

`scene-lighting.ts` bounds conventional light slots before native PBR/Standard/CEL and surface NodeMaterial shaders compile, independently of shadow allocation. The pinned Babylon adapter queries WebGL2 vertex/fragment uniform-block limits, binding count, and combined stage limits, reserving three slots for Material/Scene/Mesh. Every light declares a block in both shader stages, including unshadowed points. Missing queries use WebGL2 minima; context restoration re-queries. The non-UBO fallback retains four conventional lights. Small scenes keep Babylon's four-slot variant when the device budget permits. Unlit surfaces and overlay materials retain their own behavior.

When requested lights exceed that budget, scene-local admission retains the sun/global fill, then uses explicit Babylon `renderPriority`, camera distance with a 15% incumbent bonus (Rectangular Area Lights measure from their parent-resolved world position), and stable light identity. `shadowPriority` remains exclusive to shadow allocation. Zero-intensity, authored-disabled, and parent-disabled lights consume no slot. Effective exclusion preserves authored Enabled so camera movement, priority, intensity, or restored capacity can promote the light again. Ranking refreshes world transforms for excluded parented lights too, so parent movement or light detachment cannot leave stale selection distances. Selection runs before rendering and prewarming, including for late imported materials; changes invalidate frozen shader readiness. Render diagnostics report admitted/requested counts, the limiting slot budget, and excluded names; light helpers show these limits in yellow. This first safety policy selects by the scene's active camera, skips sorting below capacity, and does not implement clustered lighting or per-mesh selection.

Light/material additions, removals, and effective enabled-state changes trigger a batched update before rendering or shader prewarm. Each frame scans light eligibility and effective membership, sorting candidates only above capacity; material updates remain driven by membership changes, material dirtiness, and the Scene lighting flag. Updates refresh frozen material readiness without removing the editor's freeze policy; opening a small scene does not compile a large fixed light budget.

World Prefab Preview opts into `CreateEngineOptions.previewLighting`, using the same session-only key/fill lights as Material and Model Preview. PBR shading therefore remains visible without adding Light components to the prefab. Unlit shows the authored base color and textures; returning to PBR restores lighting, including frozen surface shaders. Preview lights survive prefab refreshes and are disposed with their Scene. Scene viewport and Play keep authored lighting; overlay prefabs remain unlit.

Viewport shading changes also discard frozen NodeMaterial draw caches. Otherwise an asynchronous GPU compilation can retain the previous shader through hot swapping and then freeze that fallback indefinitely. The material, textures, authored flags, freeze policy, and hot-swap preference remain intact.

## Engine default material

Meshes with no authored surface Material render the engine default, not Babylon’s white `StandardMaterial`. `installEngineDefaultMaterial` (`packages/render/src/default-material.ts`) sets `scene.defaultMaterial` to a lit `PBRMaterial` matching a new user Material (opaque, not two-sided, `metallic` 0, `roughness` 0.5) with a UV-tiled 2×2 grey checker albedo (0.8 / ~0.65, wrap, nearest, 8 tiles). Installed from `setupDefaultViewport` (editor, Play, Prefab, player), `createTestEngine`, and Material Preview. Primitives keep `mesh.material === null` so Details still shows **None**. 2D scenes have no lights, so `applySceneEnvironment` (editor and Play) also sets the checker as the default's emissive texture with white emissive color in `viewportMode: "2d"` and clears it in 3D (`syncEngineDefaultMaterialEmission`); it leaves `unlit` to the viewport Unlit shading mode. Sprites, tilemaps, skybox, 3D text, colliders, billboards, and pivot markers keep their own materials. Particle / post-process **None** is unchanged.

Model MeshComponent **Material** initially shows the imported color-map material name for a single slot, or **Model Materials** for several slots. Picking a Material overrides the scene component in the editor, Play and Preview Play. Picking **None** uses the same checker as primitives, including after a delayed model load. Reset or **Model Materials** in the picker restores all Model slots. These scene/prefab edits never change the Model asset's slot defaults; Model Editor slot None still restores the original glTF material. Serialized `materialSource: "override"` distinguishes an explicit component None (`materialGuid: null`) from legacy/inherited null (`materialSource` absent or `"model"`). A nonempty `materialGuid` remains a whole-component override for older scenes. The picker’s Model Materials value is UI-only and is never saved as an asset guid.

Prefab instances track Material GUID and source as one override: explicit None survives later prefab material edits, and resetting to the prefab selection clears both override keys. Named Material and Model Materials picks remove redundant source markers.

## Skybox mesh

`createSkyboxMesh` (editor `createMeshForComponent` and Play `createPlayMesh`) matches Babylon’s documented skybox, but does **not** call `scene.createDefaultSkybox` (that helper can write IBL and is not incremental):

- `MeshBuilder.CreateBox` at component `size` (default **1000**)
- Unlit `PBRMaterial`: `backFaceCulling = false`, `disableLighting = true`, `twoSidedLighting = true`; `reflectionTexture` in `Texture.SKYBOX_MODE`
- Mesh flags: `ignoreCameraMaxZ`, `applyFog = false` (Babylon 9 fog flag is on the mesh), `receiveShadows = false`, **always** `isPickable = false`, `renderingGroupId` background. **Not** `infiniteDistance` — position/rotation/scale come from the Skybox actor world transform (component `size` is the box edge length)
- Authored perspective cameras keep their explicit render/preview aspect while allowing Babylon to recalculate depth for `ignoreCameraMaxZ`. A size-10,000 skybox remains visible with Far Clip 1,000; ordinary geometry retains the authored clipping range after the skybox draw. Skybox materials disable depth writes so their infinite-far depth cannot cover geometry inside Far Clip.
- Missing authored faces fall back to the matching engine default cubemap face (`engine-content/skybox/{face}.png`)
- Scene and preview skyboxes retain their textured background in PBR, Unlit, and Wireframe viewport modes. The session shading overlay skips them: Babylon's PBR `unlit` flag removes the reflection path used to display the cubemap.
- Shadows, nav bake, and `frameActor` skip skyboxes (`isSkyboxMesh`; framing a 1000-unit backdrop is a no-op)
- Dispose the mesh when the actor is removed; rebuild when size or face guids change
- Six-face cubes are Engine-owned; skybox materials null `reflectionTexture` on dispose so Play overlay `scene.dispose()` / material teardown cannot drop a ResourceCache cube the editor still uses
- Authors produce custom faces with Content Browser **Skybox Creator** (editor-only helper, not a Skybox document). Preview's toolbar holds the primary Create button (disabled without a source) and a status line ("N of 6 Faces Created"); without a source it shows a **No Texture** empty state with **Pick Texture**. Net faces are labelled Up / Left / Front / Right / Back / Down with Babylon axis chips (+X … −Z); unused net cells are dimmed. Details lists **Generated Faces** (n / 6) rows only once a face exists, and Cubemap shows **No Cubemap** until then. Preview letterboxes a 4×3 net; CSS grid cells **stretch to fill** it so each compass face is square (¼ × ⅓ of the net). Decode the source Texture once (sniff PNG/JPEG MIME for the overlay blob; skip KTX2/`pixels` stubs and use `source`). Overlay placement undos merge as `skybox-creator-source`. That overlay is what Create crops; **Create Skybox Textures** writes six uncompressed `usage: "skybox"` Textures, commits face guids on the helper **before** registry refresh (so Save All cannot miss them), and surfaces registry errors as-is. **Cubemap** re-slices from the cached RGBA when placement changes. Assign those guids on `SkyboxComponent`. Equirect-to-cubemap projection is out of scope.

## 3D Text mesh

`createText3DMesh` (editor `createMeshForComponent` and Play `createPlayMesh`) is a **flat** triangulated TypeFace mesh, not GUI `TextBlock`, not a `DynamicTexture` plane, and not extruded `CreateText` cubes:

- `CreateTextShapePaths` + `CreatePolygon` (`FRONTSIDE`) + `Mesh.MergeMeshes`; inject `earcut` (Babylon does not bundle it)
- One `StandardMaterial`: unlit, two-sided (`backFaceCulling = false`), emissive color (default white)
- `fontData` is facetype.js JSON: Font chunk `facetype-glyphs` when `fontAssetGuid` is set, else the bundled ASCII TypeFace in `packages/render/src/default-typeface.ts` (5×7 pixels greedy-merged into rectangles)
- Defaults: `text` `"Text"`, `size` `1`, `color` white, `alignment` `"left"`. Serialized `depth` still parses for old scenes but does not affect the mesh or fingerprint
- `\n` starts a new line (CRLF is normalized to `\n`). Extra lines sit **above** the last line. The mesh origin stays on the **bottom** of the block
- `alignment` (`left` / `center` / `right`) only changes the horizontal bake of the combined bbox (`min X = 0`, mid X = 0, or `max X = 0`). Pivot stays on the bottom
- Empty/failed paths fall back to a plane of `size`; Details shows **Typeface** / bundled note when the Font has no facetype chunk. Text uses `MultilineTextField` (not a single-line PropertyGrid row)
- Fingerprint includes `text` / `size` / `color` / `font` / `alignment` so Details edits rebuild
- Not Development Only; ships in every export

See [fonts.md](fonts.md).

## Overlay 2D Text mesh

`createText2DMesh` (SceneLayer editor viewport and Play `assignMesh` `2dtext` / `2drichtext`) is **not** GUI `TextBlock`, not a `DynamicTexture` plane, and not a solid unlit letter box:

- Invisible wrap-box pick plane (`wrapWidth`/`wrapHeight` px ÷ `pixelsPerUnit`); glyph / `[img]` children are not pickable. Left/right Alignment uses wrap-box edges when `wrapWidth` > 0; Vertical Alignment Top / Center / Bottom pins the block in the wrap height
- Bitmap: canvas `FontFace` cells packed onto one `RawTexture` atlas when the paint is letter-shaped; a full-canvas fill or a solid tofu box (headless/software) falls back to bundled 5×7. Quad size follows the raster cell so 5×7 is not stretched to `measureText`. Quads sample `emissiveTexture` + `diffuseTexture` with alpha test, matching sprites. `setMeshAssets` supplies `fontCssStack` / `fontCssStackByGuid` from `compileText2DFontStacks`
- MSDF: imported JSON + PNG through `ResourceCache`; missing glyphs fall back to Bitmap cells. Glyphs of one text share one bundle-owned material per color/outline style
- Fingerprint includes compiled CSS stack **values** so a fallback change rebuilds meshes

See [scene-layers.md](scene-layers.md) and [fonts.md](fonts.md).

## Play debug draw

`play-debug-draw.ts` applies `{ type: "debugDraw" }` in Play (editor overlay and packed player). World-space wireframes, not editor gizmos:

- Thin segments batch into one `CreateLineSystem` per command and color; **GreasedLine** when Line thickness > 1
- `renderingGroupId` is `RENDERING_GROUP.world` (same as Play models). Play `createEngine` calls `configureEditorRenderingGroups` so group 1 does not clear depth and paint debug as an underlay. Console `showcollision` / `shownav` overlays use the same world group
- Tag `playDebugOverlay` so illumination/shadows skip them (same idea as other Play helpers)
- Duration `0` = one **sim tick** (keep across extra engine presents on overlay Play and Preview Build; `noteSimTick` from a published `pushSnapshot` expires the previous tick after it has presented). A `debugDraw` from a new runtime tick (a different `frameId`) replaces the previous tick's duration-0 meshes so the line does not blank between worker messages; duration-0 draws sharing a `frameId` accumulate (a Line Trace line and its hit marker), and a command without a numeric `frameId` replaces every duration-0 draw. Thick GreasedLine draws dispose their auto-created material on expiry. `> 0` = seconds.
- Coordinate System draws RGB axes and ignores Color
- Player `applyPlayerEngineCommand` accepts `debugDraw` **without** `bundleDebugger`

Print HUD is DOM (`print-hud` in `@babylonslate/core`), not this overlay.

## Models, skeletons, and clips

Canonical Model bytes are always GLB/glTF. Play and preview register the glTF 2.0 loader plus `KHR_materials_unlit` / `KHR_texture_transform` / `KHR_draco_mesh_compression` / `EXT_meshopt_compression` / `KHR_mesh_quantization` / `KHR_texture_basisu` (`gltf-loader.ts`) — not OBJ/STL/FBX. Draco and meshopt decoder files are self-hosted under `apps/editor/public/draco/` and `public/meshopt/` (`configureGltfMeshDecoders` in `createEngine` / `createAppEngine`; never a CDN). Packed Play uses `numWorkers: 0` for Draco like KTX2. That main-thread setting is page-global and lasts only while a Play view is live (views mounted meanwhile keep it): releasing the last Play view restores worker decoding (KTX2 best effort once Babylon has initialized its decoder), and an unchanged configuration keeps the warmed Draco decoder. Editor OBJ import converts first (`convertObjToGlb` on a throwaway Scene, preferably the shared editor Engine so texture export works; unit tests may use `NullEngine` for geometry-only OBJ). Sidecar image URIs (a pack GLB pointing at `Textures/….png`) go through Content Browser `embedGltfImportBatch` and `ingestGltfForImport` (`embedGlbExternalImages` plus strip unmatched URIs) into the stored Model BIN so Play/preview do not fetch them. A `.gltf` with a relative buffer `uri` and no matching `.bin` sidecar **throws** instead of writing an empty-BIN GLB. Model Preview keeps the placeholder scene when `LoadAssetContainerAsync` fails so authored colliders still draw, and shows **Failed to Load Mesh**. Scene/Play instantiate still leaves the empty named root and `console.warn`s `[render] Model <guid> failed to load`. **These paths are for every Model** — skinned, hierarchy, or static — not a single character pack. 3D Empty happens to scaffold Kenney Mannequin as the default actor.

glTF character content is two rigs:

| Kind | Source | Play | Bone preview | Retarget |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `skin` | `skins[]` + `JOINTS_0` / `WEIGHTS_0` | Native paused groups on the actor Model | Bone positions from each skin, with hidden-mesh skeleton updates | `AnimatorAvatar.retargetAnimationGroup` at rest pose |
| `hierarchy` | Parented meshes, no skins | Native groups already target part **Mesh** nodes | World-space joints and parent connections from the node tree, including Mesh nodes; no synthetic skin on the character | Same API after `withTransformNodeAnimationTargets` / `retargetAnimationGroupWithMeshProxy` (Mesh → same-name `TransformNode` proxy). Stock Babylon helpers skip Mesh nodes. |

Root-only object clips, two independent animated meshes, and static meshes create no Skeleton. Browse import (`classifyGltfRig`) requires parented mesh parts that share an ancestor; `boneNames` is that tree, not every mesh in the file. Do not invent vertex weights. Native Play/preview of a hierarchy Model does not retarget and does not skin the character meshes.

Helpers live in `@babylonslate/render` (`node-rig.ts`) because `@babylonslate/assets` cannot import Babylon. Bone previews, the retarget linked skeleton and hierarchy bone attachments share one joint filter (`isRigJointNode`) that skips `__root__`, `__importScale`, the hidden `materialPreviewMesh` placeholder, `*_overlay`, cameras and lights. Skeleton / Animation previews attach to `previewRigRoot` (first glTF child of that placeholder). Content Browser Retarget probes via `animationRetargetHasMatches(engine, …)` which owns a throwaway Scene. Editor previews reuse `attachMaterialPreviewGestures` and `aimPreviewCameraAtMesh`.

Skeleton assets show bones only. Animation **Show Bones** hides the model and displays the same cyan bone connectors and white joints while the clip keeps looping; switching it off restores the original mesh visibility. `attachSkeletonPreview` keeps source nodes enabled for animation, updates debug geometry in world space each render, and releases its geometry/materials and restores visibility on disposal. Rigs with no bones keep their original model visibility.

Gameplay AnimationGraph state changes seek each paused clip within its authored `from`–`to` frame range. A clip whose first key is after frame zero advances normally; normalized time zero selects that first key. Departing clips restore their original animated channels before the incoming pose is applied, so returning from Walk to Idle does not retain a hip offset that Idle never animates. This reset is scoped to the owning actor's clips and keeps live animatables paused. Jump To State uses the wired AnimationGraphComponent and resolves the selected state's Animation asset and clip name before rendering (see [Animation Graph runtime](anim-graph.md#runtime)).

### Automatic LOD

Models get Babylon mesh LOD levels automatically (`model-lod.ts`). Texture streaming is not implemented. Babylon 9.29 has no mip streaming or residency API, and swapping resolutions would need its internal texture fields.

- **Generation.** The first Auto LOD actor of a model simplifies each eligible mesh with meshoptimizer. The simplification itself is Babylon-free (`model-lod-simplify.ts`).
  - One shared module worker (`model-lod.worker.ts`) simplifies queued models one at a time. It exits when the queue drains, returning the simplifier's memory, and after any failure.
  - When workers are unavailable or fail, simplification runs on the main thread in short steps. A mesh that cannot be simplified keeps full detail without affecting the rest of its model.
  - Eligible meshes are indexed triangle meshes with at least 1024 triangles, GPU skinning, and no glTF instances or thin instances.
  - Up to three cascaded levels are error-driven: each stays within about 2 px of a 1080 px view at its switch point. Normals and UVs are weighted, and open borders are locked so separately switching parts do not crack.
  - Static meshes can go down to 1% of their triangles. Skinned and morphing meshes keep 50/25/12.5% floors because their error is measured in bind pose.
  - A level must remove at least 20% of the previous level's triangles.
  - Generated indices are cached per model bytes while any Scene uses them, plus the 32 most recently released models, so the editor viewport, Play, previews and SceneLayers share them. Levels are not persisted or exported; the player generates them after boot.
- **Index-only levels.** A level is a Babylon `Geometry` that wraps the model's own GPU vertex buffers without owning them (on WebGPU, the aligned copy) and adds its own index buffer, using the source index width.
  - Skinning, morph targets, tangents, extra UV sets and material defines therefore match the master.
  - Extra memory is index data only, and it counts toward the Scene's accounted geometry bytes.
  - One level Geometry per Scene is shared by every actor. It is released with its last LOD mesh and rebuilt from the cached indices; the model's vertex buffers are unaffected, including after a context restore.
- **Attachment.** `beginSlotModelAnimLoad` starts generation with the model load and publishes the instance at full detail, so model readiness never waits for simplification. Levels attach when generation finishes, if that instance is still published.
  - Levels share the master's vertex buffers, materials and index width, so their shader variants are already compiled. A wireframe or point material on the actor draws them through Babylon's own topology conversion.
  - Each level is a child `Mesh` of its master, attached with `Mesh.addLODLevel` and `useLODScreenCoverage`. It is unpickable and is disposed with the actor.
  - Material changes reach levels through `onMaterialChangedObservable`.
  - A Scene `onBeforeRender` pass mirrors receive-shadows, rendering group, alpha index, visibility, layer mask, side orientation, skeleton, morph targets and non-uniform scaling.
- **Selection.** A Scene `customLODSelector` runs in every ObjectRenderer pass (FrameGraph object passes, shadow maps, outline masks).
  - It uses Babylon's screen-coverage formula with the aspect normalized to 1, so a bound shadow-map target cannot change the choice. Orthographic cameras use their vertical extent.
  - It applies 10% screen-size hysteresis per camera.
  - Level thresholds are screen sizes (bounding-sphere diameter / view height) of 0.5, 0.25 and 0.125, scaled by **LOD Distance Scale**.
- **Consumers.**
  - Level meshes are `isBlocked`. They are excluded from `visualMeshes` (nav bake, outlines, slots, framing), editor material overrides, pickability writers, shadow-caster admission, water contact, live mesh counts and shadow diagnostic captures.
  - Shadow maps draw the selected level through the master caster.
  - Local shadow maps keep caching auto-LOD casters. A map re-renders once when a caster within its light's range changes its drawn level.
  - `RenderDiagnostics.autoLod` and the stats HUD report visible auto-LOD meshes, how many draw a simplified level, and the triangles saved.
- **Settings.**
  - Per Model, **Auto LOD** (`ModelPayload.autoLod`, default on) controls whether levels are generated. Changing it re-realizes loaded actors.
  - The project Scalability **Geometry** group has **Auto LOD** and **LOD Distance Scale** (Low 0.5, Medium 1, High 1.5, Ultra 2; range 0.25–4). Both apply live to selection. Off draws full detail but keeps generated levels loaded.
  - SceneLayers follow the world Scene's Geometry settings.
  - A project saved before this group existed takes the tier shared by its saved Resolution, Textures, Post Processing and Lighting groups, otherwise Medium.
- **Limits.**
  - Model Preview, thumbnails and foliage batches stay at full detail.
  - LOD reduces vertex and triangle work, not draw calls: materials remain per actor.
  - There is no cross-fade.

See [asset-registry.md](asset-registry.md) and [anim-graph.md](anim-graph.md).

Authored lights and cameras retain actor-plus-component identity. Play carries
their properties on `assignMesh.parts` alongside ordinary visuals, so several
lights or cameras can share an actor without replacing its mesh. Each native
helper follows its component hierarchy, including spring-arm sockets; the
Default Camera selects the saved component ID. Property-only illumination
updates retain working visuals and camera identities, while component removal
or visual retirement disposes every owned native helper.

Rendering admission continues applying requested shadow settings and preparing
replacement resources while shaders are unready. A failed strict probe cannot
strand the previous shadow profile; drawing and dynamic geometry uploads still
wait for successful scene admission. Admission distinguishes missing or stale
resources from a temporary readiness check on current resources: only the former
starts asynchronous preparation, so the latter can present its next ready frame.
Renderer diagnostics also snapshot scheduler and view admission gates, making a
paused or obstructed viewport distinguishable from shader/resource waiting.

## AudioService (P16)

Main-thread owner shared by overlay Play and `apps/player`. Wraps Babylon 9 AudioV2 behind `AudioPlaybackBackend`. Unit tests use `FakeAudioPlaybackBackend`; `babylon-audio-backend.ts` is coverage-excluded (needs a real audio context).

`EngineHandle.setAudioReverbField(bytes)` replaces the active scene's baked field;
pass `null` for a scene without a bake. Engine disposal detaches all owned canvas
input handlers, including overlay wheel scrolling, even when the host retains
the canvas for another session.

- Audio metadata and selected clip bytes follow asset scopes. Required scene audio and explicit preloads prepare through the shared source loader; unused Audio assets remain unread. `createEngine` still accepts already-prepared `audioBytes` for hosts and tests. `setLibrary` sanitizes missing channel/attenuation refs and cyclic channel parents. Scene `audioReverb` extra chunks feed `setReverbField`. Project Settings audio (occlusion + reverb scales) feed `setProjectAudioSettings`.
- Worker emits `playSound` / `stopSound` / `setChannelVolume` / `setGlobalVolume` only (`emitterActorGuid` / `voiceId` identity). `playSound` loops when the command **or** the Audio asset sets `loop`. Set Channel / Set Global update voices that are already playing. Spatial voices follow interpolated snapshot poses; the listener is `scene.activeCamera` (possessed, else Default Camera, else Play fallback) synced once per applied snapshot (world position and orientation) — pose and listener sync live in the deduplicated snapshot-apply path, not render admission, so a frame-cap-skipped draw still syncs exactly once. Doppler `playbackRate` uses snapshot dt only, composed with authored pitch. `AudioPlaybackBackend.onVoiceEnded` fires when a non-looping voice finishes (Babylon `onEndedObservable`; Fake `finish()`).
- First `pointerdown` / `touchstart` calls `unlockAsync()` and drains a bounded ordered queue (cap 32). Decode / missing-context diagnose without crashing Play.
- `AudioBufferCache` is a separate **256 MiB** (Engine Settings, default on; 64 MiB iPad suggestion) PCM LRU with active-voice pins, separate from `ResourceCache`. Decode keys include asset GUID, clip chunk and source revision, so replacing source bytes cannot reuse an obsolete decoded buffer. LRU eviction disposes the AudioV2 `StaticSoundBuffer`. Audio document Preview does not use this cache.
- Channels with `environmentReverb.enabled` send to one shared parametric bus; the listener interpolates wet / decay / damping, then project scales multiply. Channel-less stays dry. Failed bake → marked dry fallback, wet 0.
- Channels with `muffleThroughWalls.enabled` add a shared ~700 Hz lowpass send mixed by occupancy DDA (project Occlusion master). Extra send only — do not `disconnect()` AudioV2 `_outNode`.
- Test-mode `window.__babylonslateAudioStats` (`audioStats`) exposes `unlocked`, `queued`, `voices`, `lastGain`, `lastDistance`, `wet`, `accountedBytes`. Play open/close must return voices/buffers to baseline.

See [audio.md](audio.md).

## ParticleService

Main-thread owner shared by overlay Play, the packed player and particle Previews (`packages/render/src/particle-service.ts`). Billboard quads only; no second renderer.

- **Per-emitter backend:** a Basic emitter gets `GPUParticleSystem` (`emitRateControl: true`) when the owning engine supports compute or transform feedback, else CPU `ParticleSystem` with `min(capacity, 512)`. The owned GPU system claims its whole slot ring after construction and `reset()`, behind a runtime layout guard; at capacity the GPU recycles the oldest particle and the CPU drops new ones.
- **Particle Graph slots** are always CPU `ParticleSystem`s at the authored capacity, built from Node Particle blocks (`particle-graph-realize.ts`, `particle-graph-blocks.ts`):
  - The service lowers the document with `@babylonslate/particle-graph`, caching per document object, then builds it synchronously inside `prepare` into one `NodeParticleSystemSet` per slot. Blocks are constructed directly, never through `Parse` or `buildAsync`, and settings are written on a `SystemBlock` subclass before the build.
  - Validator errors skip the slot with `particle.graph_invalid`; Babylon build failures skip it with node-anchored `particle.compile.*` and leave no system or texture.
