That is six or seven kernel evaluations per query where inverting against the kernel at every step took about fifteen, and the height agrees with that per-step inversion to within 0.02 mm (worst measured: 14 µm with 1.5 m waves at Steepness 1 across lake, ocean and river seams).
  - Height, slope and velocity follow the blended rest height, current and swell scales. Slopes of the blended rest height and swell scale are central differences, 5 cm apart, read from the same stencil.
  - Only the owner answers, so the world finds one surface at a seam, and floating objects ride from one body into the next. A body whose margin is clearly negative at its first stencil's centre (below −(0.05 + 0.1/R)) gives up there after two kernel evaluations; only the hairline either side of a hand-over takes the full evaluation.
  - A query or buoyancy filtered to one water actor reads that actor's blended surface wherever it reaches. Blending therefore makes filtered queries evaluate every water surface; `edgeDistance` is the union shoreline distance.
- Rendering (`water-mesh.ts`, `syncSceneBlend` once per frame before placement):
  - Change detection: one Scene index of enabled surfaces, compared per frame by each surface's world matrix and enabled state while two or more surfaces exist. Created, disposed and edited surfaces mark it dirty. Each surface's blend data is keyed by numbers (the distance, its grid extension, a stamp bumped when its world matrix changes, its revision, and each neighbour's id, stamp and revision), so the check builds no strings. Only surfaces whose own placement or neighbours changed get new blend data: a level surface whose grid extension is unchanged rewrites its per-vertex rest data in place; otherwise it builds a new grid.
  - Moving neighbours: while blending surfaces keep moving, each starts a blend refresh at most every 200 ms (`WATER_BLEND_REFRESH_MS`), keeping the previous data in between; the last move always lands once the interval passes. A grid that must be rebuilt (the first blend, a new extension, a rotation out of level) never waits and is written in its frame.
  - Budgeted refresh: a level body with an unchanged grid refreshes over frames. It evaluates a frozen copy of the neighbour index and the body's pose from the frame it began in, in chunks of 32 vertices (a Global grid: only the rows near a neighbour), into staging buffers kept per surface. The surface keeps drawing its previous data and uploads nothing until every vertex is done; the rest normals' fit runs after the kernel chunks, since it reads neighbours. The scene shares a 2 ms per-frame budget (`WATER_BLEND_FRAME_BUDGET_MS`): each refresh advances at least one chunk a frame, a refresh too big for the budget advances an even share of its chunks so it lands within `WATER_BLEND_DEADLINE_MS` (100 ms, or two frames when frames are slow), and an overdue one finishes in its frame. It then swaps in and uploads once, with the bytes a write at once gives. A body that moves again meanwhile does not restart it (the frozen copy is consistent): it lands, and the next refresh, from the latest pose, starts once 200 ms have passed since the previous start. A Global recentre or a grid rebuild drops a running refresh and writes at once. Physics and worker queries (`WaterBlendIndex`, `sampleWaterBlend`) never read the mesh data, so they follow every pose immediately.
  - Grid: a finite body with a finite neighbour extends its rest grid by R/2. Lakes, puddles and oceans extend all round; a river widens only the reaches and caps within its half width plus 2R of a neighbour.
  - `placeBlend` evaluates the kernel at every vertex. It lifts the rest grid to the blended rest height, writes the union shoreline as the bank distance (`slateWaterData.y`, negative outside the union) with the blended Depth, current and rest normals, and fills the `slateWaterBlend` attribute: height scale, offset scale, ownership margin and partner-colour share. The margin is stored as an approximate signed distance in metres: the kernel's margin over the length of its gradient, fitted over the grid neighbours.
  - Global Water with neighbours repeats this when it recentres or a neighbour changes, but only over the grid rows that come within a neighbour's reach now or did at its last write (each row is a straight line in world space, so its end points bound it), plus one row either side for the rest normals' fit. Those rows run the kernel and upload in place (`updateDynamicVertexBuffer` at their byte offset); the rest keep open water's uniform rest data. The positions of those rows (their blend lift) upload with them; a recentre uploads nothing for the rest of the grid, as without blending, so a Global grid whose neighbours are out of reach uploads nothing. Normals upload once per write.
  - Shader (`SLATE_WATER_BLEND`, compiled only into surfaces with neighbours, every tier, ALU only): the vertex swell, Gerstner offset and its fade length scale by the attribute. The fragment scales the swell amplitudes, slope sums, sea state, wave height and FFT gain the same way, so it shades as the mixed body.
  - The cut code discards fragments outside the union or not owned. The margin keeps a 1.5 cm overlap, wider than its interpolation error across the two triangulations (about 1 cm on a 1 m grid), so seams never open. Both surfaces are the same there, and the strip is narrower than a pixel at most distances, so its double blending on alpha-blended tiers does not show.
  - Colours (`slateWaterShallow`, `Deep`, `Foam`, `Absorb`, `Through`) mix toward the partner's (`slateWaterBlend*` uniforms) by the vertex's partner share. Toon hands them over in three flat cels (soft-edged steps of the share) rather than an airbrushed gradient.
  - The terrain field's rest heights follow the blended surface; culling bounds pad by the largest blended swell scale.
  - A blend whose rest height varies (neighbours at different rest heights) is not flat (`waterMeshRestVaries`), so it takes no planar mirror and reflects like any other unmirrored body at Planar (the screen-space march when the copy exists, the sky otherwise).
- Cost when unused: distance 0 or a single surface returns before any per-frame work, and a body with no neighbours compiles, uploads and samples exactly as before. Two or more surfaces cost one 16-float matrix comparison per surface per frame. Blending surfaces add one vec4 attribute, a varying, about 20 ALU per fragment and none per vertex beyond three multiplies.
- Known limits:
  - The owner's Style and look shade the seam, and only its colours blend; a Realistic body beside a Stylized one meets in a visible change of style.
  - One partner's colours per surface: where a third differently coloured body takes part, its colour reads as the partner's.
  - Custom Material water has no ownership discard, so overlapping custom surfaces still both draw.
  - A blended current varies across the seam, and the shader advects its chop and foam patterns by current × time. As at a river's bends, the pattern there shears more the longer the clock runs.
  - The vertical fade is measured from the evaluating body, so three or more bodies at different heights can disagree slightly at a seam.
  - The editor outline mask displaces blending water by its own swell, not the blended scale.

### Water shading per tier

Built-in water compiles one shader per Water Shading Detail (`SLATE_WATER_QUALITY`). Physics, the vertex swell and every asset setting are the same on all tiers; only per-pixel detail changes. Terms a tier drops add their slope variance to the filtered roughness, so Low reads as Medium water seen from further away, not as a glassier sea.

| Term | Low | Medium | High | Ultra |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Swell components in the fragment normal (all at the warped rest point, each with its wave group's envelope) | 3 largest slopes (+ skipped variance) | 6 largest slopes (+ skipped variance) | all 8, + the envelopes' slope | all 8, + the envelopes' slope |
| Realistic chop octaves / capillaries | 2 / 0 | 4 / 1 | 6 / 4 | 6 / 4, extra capillary patching |
| Realistic transmittance | one channel + drawn wet-sand bed light (per channel) | per channel (Shallow Color absorption) | per channel | per channel |
| Realistic bed caustics | off | off | shimmer field (ALU) | as High |
| Octave footprint | isotropic | isotropic | per direction | per direction |
| Fine chop-domain bend | — | on | on | on |
| Shared noises (+ contact ripple noise), each lattice turned to its own fixed angle | 2 (others derived) | 4 + 1 | 4 + 1 | 4 + 1 |
| Chop domain warp | sized to Low's derived gust | full | full | full |
| Realistic foam pattern | foam clumps broken by one ridged noise, stretched along the wind, into streaky lace; caps without dense cores; the faintest foam fades out | clumps, blobs, 1 bubble web (twice the large scale) | churned web + small-bubble web (3 web evaluations) | as High |
| Breaking zones | large noise inverted, broken by the medium one | derived | own noise | own noise |
| Whitecap trails | off | on | on | on |
| Realistic Surface Foam streaks | wind streaks (ALU) | wind streaks | wind streaks | wind streaks |
| Contact foam | band and line, stretched downstream | full patches, stretched downstream | + trailing wake (2 contact taps) | as High |
| Contact ripple agitation | off | on | on | on |
| Shore swash (every look) | run-up and drain, cover and swash front; film dries through the backwash | + stranded line, film aged since the edge passed (two cycles), bores through the surf | as Medium | as Medium |
| Contact swell (every look) | collar pulse and cling, ripple bursts | + lee puffs | as Medium | as Medium |
| Sparkles (when Sparkles > 0) | off | on | on | on |
| Realistic sun | unlit: one analytic lobe on a half-flattened normal, stretched along the view, log-compressed, widened by the pixel's normal variation and gathered into world-anchored glitter (ALU) | GGX (log-compressed, widened by the pixel's normal variation) + filtered close facet glints + world-anchored glitter beyond | as Medium (glitter dashes to the horizon) | as High |
| Realistic scene lights | none (unlit) | scene capacity | scene capacity | scene capacity |
| Stylized chop octaves (both looks) | 1 | 2 | 3 | as High |
| Painted ripples | two gated triangle-wave strokes and a crossing wavelet | two gated capillary stroke families, plus a drifting gate noise | as Medium | as High |
| Painted sky | analytic | + 3 scene-copy taps (views with a copy) | + 4 more taps | as High |
| Painted foam | one soft lace octave, the swash front | two lace octaves and a far octave, lace on each bore, ripple wisps, drifting streaks | + round holes, finer octave | as High |
| Painted sun | marks (two footprint-stepped octaves), sheen, halo | + star sparkles | as Medium | as High |
| Toon lines and Subsurface | two crest isolines (swell only), one fixed scale of close strokes and dapples riding the water, flecks; top tone | + trough isoline, footprint-stepped close strokes and dapples, backlit crest band | as Medium | as High |
| Toon horizon | wave-following far steps, four distance haze bands to the horizon tone | + haze to the sky's colour (2 scene-copy taps and the pixel's own horizon tap, views with a copy) | + 2 more taps | as High |
| Toon foam | shore band (scallops from the medium noise) and breathing ring, collars and two rings, caps | + finer scallops and collar lobes, a line on each bore, lee puffs, drifting patches | + finer lobes, 3 × 3 point holes | as High |
| Toon sun | narrower column of dashes | column of dashes | + star sparkles | as High |
| Refraction (Refraction > 0, views with a scene copy) | off: blended surface | half-resolution copy | 0.75-resolution copy | full-resolution copy |
| Object reflections (Object Reflections on) | sky | sky | 16-step screen-space march | planar mirror for the dominant flat body; 24-step march for other bodies |

- Low lighting: Realistic water at Low sets `disableLighting`, as Stylized water always does, so its shader has no PBR light loop or shadow taps however many lights the scene has (a budget phone otherwise paid at least four light slots per water pixel). The water shader already estimates the strongest directional light and hemispheric ambient (`slateWaterSun`, `slateWaterLight`), so the sun lobe and foam lighting come from those; the environment reflection stays. Point, spot and area lights do not light Low water. Switching back to a lit tier invalidates the scene's light-slot capacity, so the material regains every slot. The plugin owns this flag per tier and restores it after the editor's Unlit viewport mode, which owns it while active.
- Low keeps the same look from fewer terms: breaking zones come from the medium noise (Low's gust is its large noise, and a zone derived from that cancelled the gust, so every steep crest broke along its whole length), the chop warp's second component is scaled to the substituted noise's frequency. Painted Low draws its ripples as smoothed triangle waves that fade sooner, shades its single chop octave less and unevenly (and drifts its phase half as much), so it never draws regular stripes or swirls, and draws fewer, coarser caps. Toon Low narrows its sun column (its smoother normal would otherwise spread it into one broad blob), and Toon caps raise their cut with the footprint on every tier, so distant caps shrink and vanish instead of shimmering. Realistic Low folds its one foam noise into two crossing families of curved lace bands, one bent by the medium noise and one by the chop (triangle waves: no texture fetch, no transcendental), faded to their mean where they would alias: thinning foam opens rounded holes where both bands dip and frays into strands, dense cap cores ease in and vary with the bands, and the bands stand in for foam grain, so storm foam no longer draws flat, hard-edged white blobs. Its unlit foam light is scaled to Medium's lit foam brightness (it used to clip to flat white). Realistic's per-pixel power terms (Fresnel, the horizon flattening, Subsurface) are multiplications, not `pow`, on every tier.
- Shader size per tier is listed in [the performance budget](../design/perf-budget.md). `e2e/water.spec.ts` renders the Realistic, Stylized Painted, Stylized Toon and custom-material presets in the Water preview on WebGL2 with no shader errors; tier and shore-foam pixels have no browser test.

Add **Water Buoyancy** to the object that should float. It creates a dynamic body and a box collider when needed; authored Rigid Body and collider components take precedence. Match Width, Length, Height and Offset to the hull. Rigid Body Mass controls weight. Zero Volume balances an unloaded object halfway submerged; set Volume explicitly to give the hull a fixed carrying capacity. Native Havok collision impulses transfer falling loads, allowing dipping, tipping and sinking under excess weight. This is surface buoyancy, not a fluid-volume simulation or splash solver.

Only two dedicated graph nodes are added:

| Graph | Node | Purpose |
| --- | --- | --- |
| NodeGraph | Sample Water Surface | Queries a world position and optional Water Actor. Returns Found, world surface height, signed immersion depth, surface normal, water velocity (current plus orbital X/Z motion and the surface's height rate in Y, as above), bank distance and the live Water Actor. Outside water returns Found false. Global Water Volume bank distance is infinite. |
| MaterialGraph | Water Surface | Reads world-unit vertical wave displacement (the vertex already includes any Gerstner horizontal offset), bank distance, scaled water depth, simulation time and world current from the rendered mesh, which a Custom Material keeps CPU-displaced. Ordinary meshes return zero. Global shading clamps bank distance to 10,000 metres. |

A Water asset's **Custom Material** accepts a Surface MaterialGraph; it replaces built-in shading while retaining geometry waves and buoyancy. Use Water Surface to blend foam by bank distance or animate effects with the water clock. Additional graph position offsets affect rendering only. Existing component variable Get/Set nodes expose water assignment, dimensions, current, waves, enabled state and buoyancy tuning. Water content is loaded before scene realization in Play and the player; simulated (dilated) water time travels through the worker bridge paired with snapshot frames and freezes with Play pause (see Water time above).

## Lattice Deformer

`DeformerComponent` targets one **Mesh Component** on the same actor, including imported models with skeletal or morph animation. It starts disabled; choose the target and enable it in Details. A target uses its first enabled deformer. Prefab references and duplicated actors resolve to their own component instances. Attached components and child actors remain separate deformation owners in both the editor and Play.

- Geometry order is **morph → skin/world transform → material WPO → lattice → render outputs**. The cage is expressed in the target component's local coordinates. Auto Fit derives one cage from the undeformed model parts; manual bounds permit a larger animated/WPO envelope. Vertices outside the cage retain their incoming positions.
- Babylon 9.29 `Lattice` and `LatticePluginMaterial` own interpolation and the nearest-sampled RGBA32F 3D texture. Narrow GLSL/WGSL adapters place that stock evaluator after WPO and transform normals/tangents using the same eight control-point samples. Authored surface, outline, shadow and capture variants consume the same final geometry.
- Resolution is 2–4 control points per axis (8–64 total), with a 2×2×2 default. Offsets are target-local XYZ values, flattened with X changing fastest. Changing resolution resets offsets. Strength blends offsets from 0 to 1; zero strength or all-zero offsets require no cage texture or lattice fetches.
- One cage texture is shared by a target's parts and LODs. Control edits retain shared source geometry, coalesce into revisioned worker snapshots, and update the control texture without rebuilding meshes. The texture stores 128–1,024 bytes, accounted against the shared managed rendering budget.
- Imported repeated model parts retain Babylon instancing within their component owner. Model clones have independent instance sources while sharing geometry. Direct instance groups spanning different cage owners are rejected because one instanced draw can bind only one cage; ordinary undeformed models retain their existing batching.
- Visual deformation expands culling bounds conservatively. Physics and CPU triangle picking keep their existing source geometry, as with authored shader WPO. Materials outside the engine's supported shader adapters report an error rather than silently drawing different geometry in different passes.
- Previous-frame cage controls are not retained for temporal velocity output. Effects that depend on accurate deformation motion vectors need separate integration and qualification.

The enabled vertex shader still evaluates the lattice on every affected vertex in every pass, even while controls are unchanged. Texture size is not a frame-time guarantee. `e2e/lattice-deformer.spec.ts` compares stock and adapted geometry against independent references, WPO/animation ordering, shared materials and outlines on WebGL2. Device performance and the A16 budget require measurements; no low-spec qualification is implied by this implementation.

The same spec has an explicitly gated `BL_PERF_LATTICE=1` measurement, admitted through the normal browser runner. Select `--config playwright.perf.config.ts --project perf-gpu` with `BL_RENDER_NATIVE_GPU=1` for desktop hardware measurements. It compares no deformation, stock static/updated controls and adapted static/updated controls on a 16,384-vertex mesh at 2³, 3³ and 4³ cage resolutions. Each case has a ten-second warm-up and two thirty-second samples. Reports separate control-update CPU time, submission CPU time, frame intervals and available GPU query timings. This synthetic single-pass workload does not certify animated, multi-pass or physical A16 performance; missing GPU timers and software adapters remain visible in the report.

## Dynamic Runtime Mesh

`DynamicRuntimeMeshComponent` is a world component, available from **Add Component → Rendering → Dynamic Runtime Mesh** or `Add Component` during Play. It starts empty. It has no asset type or Place Actors preset; generated geometry belongs to the running component and is discarded on clear, component destruction, scene removal, or Stop.

Drag its object reference into a NodeGraph to find its target-bound functions. Geometry uses component-local coordinates and flat numeric arrays; JavaScript callers may also supply typed arrays through `ctx.callComponentFunction(component, runtimeName, arguments)`.

| Function | Runtime Name | Inputs / Behavior |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Set Geometry | `setDynamicMeshGeometry` | `positions` (XYZ), `indices` (triangle triples), optional `normals` (XYZ) and `uvs` (UV). Replaces topology; returns `success`. Missing/empty normals are generated once; missing/empty UVs become zero. |
| Update Vertices | `updateDynamicMeshVertices` | `firstVertex` (zero-based), optional `positions`, `normals`, `uvs`. Writes contiguous ranges, retaining omitted/empty channels and topology; returns `success`. |
| Clear Geometry | `clearDynamicMeshGeometry` | Releases vertex/index storage and removes collision on the next physics step. Retains component settings and material assignment. |
| Recalculate Normals | `recalculateDynamicMeshNormals` | Explicit whole-mesh normal rebuild after deformation. Supply normals with Update Vertices to avoid this scan. |
| Recalculate Bounds | `recalculateDynamicMeshBounds` | Tightens bounds after geometry shrinks. Partial position edits otherwise expand conservative bounds by scanning only changed vertices. |

**Set Material** and **Get Material Object** use the normal component-targeted property API, including per-component material parameters. Material changes retain geometry. Cast Shadows, Receive Shadows, Enable Collision, Layer and Mask are component properties. Invalid indices, mismatched channels, non-finite values, out-of-range edits, or geometry exceeding one million vertices / six million indices are rejected before mutation. Attribute magnitudes are limited to `1e10` to keep float geometry and generated normals finite. Input arrays are copied, so callers can reuse them immediately.

Performance contract:

- Fixed-size edits and replacements reuse CPU arrays and GPU buffers. Resizing reallocates affected buffers; clear releases them. Index buffers support 32-bit vertex indices.
- Edits coalesce per component per simulation tick into bounded dirty ranges. Only changed channels cross the worker bridge, using owned transferable arrays. Clean ticks send no geometry. Play and the exported player use the same command handler.
- Rendering coalesces packets before admission into one GPU upload per changed channel. CPU data remains available for picking and graphics-context restoration; bounds and picking caches update with geometry. Revision-tracked shadows can stay cached while geometry is unchanged.
- Geometry collision is **disabled by default**. Enabling it uses the current triangles in 3D physics; no Rigid Body means an implicit static body, while an attached Rigid Body owns motion. Position/topology edits recook at the next physics step; normal, UV and material edits do not. Collision cooking is proportional to mesh size, so keep it disabled for frequently deforming visuals that do not need exact triangle collision. Collapsed triangles contribute no collision surface.

## Render-only game-time pause prerequisite

- `EngineHandle.setGameTimePaused` adds a pause reason independent of the existing caller and streaming holds. `requestPausedRedraw` admits one explicit redraw without resuming those holds; it returns a named refusal when a scene owner cannot redraw safely. These are internal integration primitives, not a completed Simulation UI or runtime pause acknowledgment.
- The FrameGraph redraw skips Babylon's animation/physics pass. Native animation wall time resets on resume. Authored shader Time/Seconds inputs, periodic/manual Render Target captures, CPU/GPU particle simulation, queued GLB weighted poses, and preview water clocks remain held. Audio voices use the existing pause owner and the listener retains its last game-camera pose. No new clock observers or recording buffers are installed.
- The pinned Babylon patch adds `GPUParticleSystem.paused`, matching the CPU owner's public pause flag. Paused GPU draws reuse completed buffers without prewarming, sampling emitter gradients, consuming manual emissions, or dispatching updates. A system with no initialized buffers remains undrawn until resume. The owner must hold this flag; otherwise paused-redraw admission reports the offending particle system and retains the last coherent image. The patch also adds `Scene.render`'s optional `ignoreActions` argument; frame and intersection actions resume from their existing state on the next gameplay presentation.
- This boundary does not pause Worker scripts, arbitrary JavaScript callbacks, or external side effects. Session ownership must first acknowledge a runtime boundary, drain accepted render commands, and manage input/editor-camera routing. Queued weighted animation commands remain pending until a real animation pass; final-state capture must resolve that ownership explicitly. GPU, browser presentation, audio, and full Simulation acceptance remain unqualified by headless checks alone.

## Saved component material instances

- Scene components may carry `materialInstance` with a material GUID and typed parameter values. Full scene loads, incremental editor reconciliation and prefab thumbnails apply these only while the component's current material assignment matches that GUID. Text keeps its existing glyph/domain binding.
- Each component visual owns a private MaterialLibrary instance key. Uniform state is private; immutable textures still use the existing resource leases. Overrides wait for the exact material and parameter texture preparation before replacing the previous valid material. Superseded requests, removed overrides, deleted actors and disposed scenes release only their own instance.
- The existing scene-load readiness path includes pending instance preparation and reports invalid parameter names/types, missing materials and texture failures. These persistence/rendering boundaries do not by themselves qualify complete Simulation retention.

- `setGameInputEnabled` independently gates SceneLayer pointer, wheel, keyboard, and programmatic focus entry. Input ownership changes cancel pressed/hovered buttons, controls, scrolling and captured pointers; repeat keys and a gesture started under another owner cannot become fresh game input. `setSimulationEditMode` resets debug-camera gestures and switches the existing free camera without releasing the host's input gate or pause reasons. Movement requests a render-only redraw while paused. Running Edit follows the possessed/default game camera for positional audio, including gameplay camera possession changes; the debug camera remains a viewport-only choice. The ordinary console free-camera behavior is unchanged.

Explicit frame reports use `EngineHandle.captureFrame()` and
`cancelFrameCapture()`. The view admits one request for ten seconds and resolves
it only after a coherent world presentation reaches that view's copy destination.
A held candidate is discarded; RTT promises carry their own generation and frame
receipt, so an old copy cannot complete a later request. Scene replacement,
context loss, disposal, cancellation, and drawing failure reject the request.
Performance recording and frame capture exclude one another. A paused request
uses the acknowledged game-time pause path and requests one render-only frame;
a presentation-only pause cannot safely substitute for that boundary. Session
hosts restrict these expensive diagnostics to normal Play and packaged Preview.

A capture attaches public FrameGraph task observers only around the requested
synchronous draw, removes them in `finally`, and records configured task/pass
branches separately from actual task execution. Task timings are inclusive wall
time, may contain driver waits, and are not per-pass or GPU timings. Public
texture handles, dimensions, formats and sample counts retain their graph
identity; absent details remain unavailable. Native submission, world output,
SceneLayer scene output/composition, editor overlays, and existing Render Target
Capture owners have explicit stages. Known capture actors are attributed by
identity; batched rendering is not guessed. The collector retains at most 256
records in each collection, 16 graph submissions, 32 passes per branch, and a
256 KiB serialized-record admission budget. Omitted records make the report
incomplete and increment its drop count. This is not browser heap accounting.
Ordinary frames allocate no report buffers or descriptors and attach no report
observers. Texture readback/previews, Spector, and GPU pass timings are absent.

A validated runtime transform edit announces its authoritative snapshot frame
with `resetActorInterpolation`. The renderer checks GUID/slot ownership and
removes the previous interpolation endpoint when that publication arrives,
including world-space descendant poses. It does not apply authored local
coordinates to the flattened render roots. Coalesced pending reset publications
use a bounded frame interval; subsequent ordinary snapshots resume interpolation.

Simulation attaches the existing gizmo host through
`EngineHandle.attachRuntimeTransformTools`. It owns one invisible draft mesh and
the viewport coordinator's existing editor-overlay slot. The actual runtime mesh
is never dragged directly: continuous and final world-pose drafts go through the
correlated runtime mutation channel, where authoritative parents and physics
apply the edit. The final draft remains displayed until its acknowledgement,
then follows the effective runtime pose. Mesh matrices already contain absolute
world coordinates; Babylon's floating-origin shader offset is not added again.
The adapter binds the full runtime identity to an explicitly supplied render slot
and its visual lifetime, refuses stale/recycled targets, and clears the selection
on destruction. A replacement visual can rebind only when the same scene-instance,
actor and component lifetime tokens still own that slot; any drag of its predecessor
is cancelled first. It uses selected-object reads only. Inspector world-pose replies
can position a gizmo for an object without a render mesh.

Final authoring capture drains the command-source queue before validating native
visual/material readiness. Already-accepted cold assignments may publish through
that fence without advancing game time. Source failure rejects capture instead
of retaining a predecessor visual; any newly received runtime command invalidates
the fence. Cancelling this wait does not assert that underlying native work ended.

Gizmo gestures and the existing free camera have exclusive pointer ownership.
Edit exit, pointer cancellation, focus loss, selection changes, and disposal
release active gestures without manufacturing a final committed write. A pointer
which began on a gizmo cannot become a camera drag later. The adapter is created
only for Simulation consumers, starts disabled, and is enabled only while the
session uses Edit input; ordinary Play does not allocate its proxy, gizmos, or
utility Scene. UI consumers retain the acknowledged object identity and expose
pending/rejected writes through the shared Inspector transport.
